# Polynomial vacuum representations and bridge mass for honeycomb walks

Coordinate `EN-115-000-0003-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M2-B-ML-M-0482` | `PM-237.9` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Polynomial-vacuum-representations-and-bridge-mass-for-honeycomb-walks-September-26-2026` | date 2026-09-26 | derived from the openai/math preprint Polynomial-vacuum-representations-and-bridge-mass-for-honeycomb-walks-September-26-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove that, under the all-length critical measure, a honeycomb bridge crossing a strip of <i>h</i> layers, with its initial port fixed and its terminal port free, has mean length $`h^{4/3+o(1)}`$. We also obtain central visit probability $`R^{-2/3+o(1)}`$ and mean length $`R^{4/3+o(1)}`$ for macroscopic free-boundary chords in a regular hexagon.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that, under the all-length critical measure, a honeycomb bridge crossing a strip of <i>h</i> layers, with its initial port fixed and its terminal port free, has mean length $`h^{4/3+o(1)}`$. We also obtain central visit probability $`R^{-2/3+o(1)}`$ and mean length $`R^{4/3+o(1)}`$ for macroscopic free-boundary chords in a regular hexagon.

- **claim-strip-bridge-mean** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-pv-bridge)): As the integer layer height $h$ tends to infinity, $$b_h=h^{-1/4+o(1)},\qquad \ell_h=h^{13/12+o(1)},\qquad \mathbb E_h\|\gamma\|=h^{4/3+o(1)}.$$ All three sums are finite. These exponents are unchanged if the paths are also confined to horizontal distance $h\log^2h$ from their initial port.
  - Explanation: Separately identifies bridge crossing mass and its first-length insertion, then obtains the normalized mean. Finiteness and preservation under the stated widening corridor are part of the result.
- **claim-hexagon-visits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-pv-hexagon)): The mass normalizing $\mathbb P_R$ is $R^{3/4+o(1)}$. Uniformly at vertices in the concentric hexagon of side $R/3$, $$\mathbb P_R(v\in\gamma)=R^{-2/3+o(1)}.$$ The corresponding upper bound holds at every vertex, and $\mathbb E_R\|\gamma\|=R^{4/3+o(1)}$.
  - Explanation: Determines the free-boundary macroscopic chord normalizer, bulk visit probability and mean length. The visit lower bound is restricted to the concentric inner hexagon; the upper bound holds throughout.
- **claim-conditioned-mixed-exits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-pv-mixed)): The conditioned event has unnormalized mass $h^{-1/4+o(1)}$ and first-length mass $h^{13/12+o(1)}$. Hence its normalized mean length is $h^{4/3+o(1)}$. Restricting horizontal distance to $h\log^2h$ preserves these exponents. The total first-length mass of all exits, without the height condition, is at most $h^{13/12+o(1)}$.
  - Explanation: Obtains mass and first-length mass for exits conditioned to reach a fixed fractional height. Their quotient gives the mixed mean, but a separate comparison is needed to isolate bridges.
- **claim-complex-separator-pairing** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-pv-puncture)): For every compact set $K\subset\{s\in\mathbb C:\|\Im s\|<\pi\}$ there is $C_K<\infty$ such that, for all $n\ge1$ and $s\in K$, $$\|F_n(2\cosh s)\|\le C_K n^{1/6+2\Re(s^2)/(3\pi^2)}.$$ There is a disk about $\eta=0$, independent of $n$, in which $F_n$ has no zeros. For each fixed $k$, the order-$k$ Taylor coefficient of $\log F_n$ at zero is $O_k(1+\log n)$. At the positive fugacity two, $$F_n(2)=n^{1/6+o(1)}.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the normalized separator polynomial uniformly on compact complex twist sets, establishes a common zero-free neighborhood, and controls fixed-order logarithmic coefficients. Positivity supplies the matching positive-fugacity exponent.
- **claim-finite-critical-flux** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pv-flux)): For an outer boundary port of a finite convex domain bounded by tiling lines, start a path inwards and let it exit anywhere. Then $$\sum_\gamma x_*^{\|\gamma\|} e^{i\sigma W(\gamma)}=1,\qquad \sigma=3/8$$ where the sum is over the nontrivial walks to exit, $\|\gamma\|$ counts vertices, and $W$ is total turning angle along honeycomb edges. Every such walk has $\|W\|\le\pi$, so its real phased weight is at least $\cos(\sigma\pi)x_*^{\|\gamma\|}$.
  - Explanation: Local extension and reversal cancellations give an exact boundary flux identity. Convexity bounds total turn and makes real phased weights comparable to absolute weights.
- **claim-strip-flux-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-pv-strip-flux)): The strip masses $A_h,b_h$ are finite and satisfy $$\label{eq:pv:strip-flux} b_h+\cos(\sigma\pi)A_h=1.$$ Moreover, $$A_{h+1}-A_h\le x_*^{-1} b_{h+1}^2, \qquad \frac{c}{h+1}\le b_h\le1.$$
  - Explanation: Passes finite-domain flux to the infinite strip and relates bridge mass to returning arch mass. Splitting an arch at its highest layer gives the difference estimate and the crude bridge bounds.
- **claim-fixed-cylinder-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pv-cylinder-decay)): Fix $N$. Let $b_h^{(N)}$ and $A_h^{(N)}$ denote the cylindrical versions of the strip masses from a fixed bottom port, with terminal port summed on the top and bottom cut respectively. Then $\sum_h b_h^{(N)}<\infty$, the limit $A_\infty^{(N)}$ is finite, and there are $C_N,c_N>0$ such that $$b_h^{(N)}+A_\infty^{(N)}-A_h^{(N)} \le C_N e^{-c_N h}.$$ The same bounds apply when a terminal port is prescribed instead of summed.
  - Explanation: At fixed circumference, cylindrical bridge weights and the remaining arch tail decay exponentially with height. The constants may depend on circumference, and prescribing a terminal only decreases the positive sums.
- **claim-local-slide-yang-baxter** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pv-local-relations)): As rational identities, the maps from $V$ and $V^{\otimes2}$, respectively, to $V^{\otimes3}$ satisfy $$F_{12}(z)F_{23}(Tz)(C\otimes1)=1\otimes C,\qquad F_{12}(z)F_{23}(t^2 z)(I\otimes1)=(1\otimes I)F(tz).$$ The checked matrices also satisfy the Yang–Baxter equation $$F_{12}(y)F_{23}(zy)F_{12}(z) =F_{23}(z)F_{12}(zy)F_{23}(y).$$
  - Explanation: Proves cup and fusion slides and the checked Yang–Baxter equation as rational identities. Degeneration values reduce the interchange equation to the slides.
- **claim-transfer-row-relations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pv-row-relations)): At fixed twist, the row transfers commute in their row arguments. Exchanging adjacent sites by $F$ intertwines the corresponding rows. At generic sites, and hence meromorphically, $$\mathcal T_{\kappa}(x_j)\mathcal T_{\kappa}(Tx_j)=1,\qquad \mathcal T_{\kappa}(x_j)\mathcal T_{\kappa}(t^2x_j)=\mathcal T_{\kappa}(tx_j).$$ For adjacent sites $(x,Tx)$, insertion of $C$ intertwines the row with the row omitting those sites. For $(x,t^2x)$, insertion of $I$ intertwines it with the row having the single site $tx$. On charge zero, $\mathcal T_\kappa(0)=(1+\kappa+\kappa^{-1})1$.
  - Explanation: Lifts local interchange and slide identities to commuting twisted rows, row fusion and site insertion. The last scalar specialization is restricted to charge zero.
- **claim-physical-common-eigenline** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pv-physical-eigenline)): At $X=(1,\ldots,1)$, $z=t$, and $\kappa=\pm i$, powers of the row transfer converge exponentially to a rank-one projection whose empty-to-empty entry is 1. In particular, eigenvalue 1 is simple and all other eigenvalues have modulus less than 1. The same conclusions hold at the reflected honeycomb point $z=t^2$.* *Near either set of physical sites and twist, the rows have a common analytic eigenline of charge zero. Its eigenvalue is rational in the row argument and analytic in the other data near regular arguments. At $\kappa=\pm i$ this row eigenvalue is identically 1.
  - Explanation: Loop cancellation and fixed-cylinder decay produce the normalized physical projection. Perturbation and commutation give a common analytic charge-zero eigenline nearby, with the stated row eigenvalue at the physical twists.
- **claim-polynomial-vacuum-construction** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-pv-vacuum)): Use twist $\zeta=t^2=i$. Let $\rho X=(x_N,x_1,\ldots,x_{N-1})$, and $\Pi_\kappa$ the corresponding rotation of spin slots with factor $\kappa^{h_N}$ from the last (now first) spin. We construct a charge-zero vector polynomial $\Psi_N(X)$ with $$F_{i,i+1}(x_i/x_{i+1})\Psi(X)=\Psi(s_i X),\qquad \Pi_\zeta\Psi(X)=\Psi(\rho X)$$ ($s_i$ adjacent transposition), homogeneous of degree $\binom N2$, with degree at most $N-1$ in each variable. For a spin component $\Psi_{N,\boldsymbol h}$, the refined bounds are $$h_j=+1\ \Longrightarrow\ \deg_{x_j}\Psi_{N,\boldsymbol h}\le N-2, \qquad h_j=-1\ \Longrightarrow\ x_j\mid\Psi_{N,\boldsymbol h}.$$ It has the adjacent-pair reduction $$\Psi_N(\ldots,a,Ta,\ldots)=s_C(a;Y)\ \iota_C \Psi_{N-2}(Y), \qquad s_C(a;Y)=(1+T)a\prod_{x\in Y}(x-t^5a)(x-t^6a),$$ where $Y$ is the remaining list and $\iota_C$ inserts a $C$ tensor in the indicated positions; and at last parameter zero its projection onto last spin zero is $(\prod_{j<N}x_j)\Psi_{N-1}$.
  - Explanation: Constructs the charge-zero polynomial vacuum by interpolation at cup and zero nodes. Refined spin-dependent degree bounds force the remaining exchange and cyclic discrepancies to vanish.
- **claim-multiaffine-discrepancy-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-auto-claim-1)): A tensor polynomial multiaffine in at least three consecutive variables, with the bit-dependent spin support and exchange relation just stated, is zero.
  - Explanation: Channel projections and the permitted spin support eliminate every sorted bit pattern; adjacent exchanges then eliminate all coefficients. This closes cyclicity in the vacuum induction.
- **claim-pfaffian-empty-component** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-pv-empty-component)): Put $D(a,b)=a^2+\sqrt2 ab+b^2$ and $K(a,b)=(a^2-b^2)/D(a,b)$. The symmetric empty component is $$S_N(X)=\prod_{i<j}\frac{D(x_i,x_j)}{x_i-x_j}\ {\rm Pf}[K(x_i,x_j)]$$ where for odd size an extra last Pfaffian index has $K_{i,N+1}=1$, $i\le N$, corresponding to parameter zero. It satisfies $$S_N(X^{-1})=\frac{S_N(X)}{\prod_i x_i^{N-1}}$$ and the second fusion identity $$S_N(a,t^2a,Y)=\sqrt2\,t a\prod_{y\in Y}(y-t^5 a)\ S_{N-1}(ta,Y).$$
  - Explanation: Identifies the symmetric empty polynomial by a Pfaffian formula, with the dummy index convention at odd size. It also establishes reciprocal parameters and the second fusion reduction.
- **claim-normalized-vacuum-reductions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-pv-normalized-reductions)): At generic parameters, the normalized states reduce by insertion of $C$ at adjacent sites $(a,Ta)$, and by insertion of $I$ at $(a,t^2a)$, replacing those sites by the single site $ta$. When one parameter tends to zero or infinity, the spin-zero component at that slot tends to the reduced $\Omega$, with scalar factor one.
  - Explanation: After empty-component normalization, cup insertion, fusion and extreme-parameter spin-zero limits have no additional scalar factor. These are generic identities and limits.
- **claim-geometric-separator-identification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-pv-physical-pairing)): For two homogeneous blocks of equal size $n$, $$X_{n,n}(1,\ldots,1;\kappa)=F_n(\kappa+\kappa^{-1}).$$ Here the left side is the bilinear vacuum contraction just defined and the right side is the positive polygon polynomial from Section <a href="#sec:pv:introduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:pv:introduction">1</a>.
  - Explanation: Identifies the homogeneous bilinear vacuum contraction with the positive polygon separator polynomial. Seam phases cancel nonseparating polygons and give the required fugacity to separating ones.
- **claim-pairing-interpolation-uniqueness** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-pv-pairing-interpolation)): Fix $\kappa\ne0$ and put $\eta=\kappa+\kappa^{-1}$. The family $X_{n,m}$ is uniquely determined among rational functions by the following properties. It is symmetric separately in its two blocks, and $S_N^2X_{n,m}$ is polynomial of degree at most $2(N-1)$ in each site, where $N=n+m$. Within block A its reductions are $$\begin{aligned} X_{n,m}(a,Ta,A';B)&=X_{n-2,m}(A';B),\\ X_{n,m}(a,t^2a,A';B)&=X_{n-1,m}(ta,A';B),\\ \lim_{a\to0\text{ or }\infty}X_{n,m}(a,A';B) &=X_{n-1,m}(A';B), \end{aligned}$$ The pair reductions require $n\ge2$, and the extreme reduction requires $n\ge1$; the analogous rules hold in block B with $m$ in place of $n$. The initial value is $X_{0,0}=1$, and at every balanced size $n=m>0$, $$X_{n,n}(a_1,\ldots,a_n;Ta_n,\ldots,Ta_1)=(1+\eta)^n.$$
  - Explanation: Characterizes the rational pairing family by block symmetry, cleared polynomial degrees, pair and extreme reductions, and balanced nested-cup data. Divisibility and degree comparison prove uniqueness.
- **claim-boundary-crossing-exponent** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-pv-boundary)): For critical bridges across $h$ layers from a fixed bottom port to all top ports, $b_h=h^{-1/4+o(1)}$. The half-plane arch mass to the right is $1/(2\cos(3\pi/8))$; its height and diameter tails both have exponent $-1/4$.
  - Explanation: Determines the crossing-mass exponent and the total rightward half-plane arch mass. Tilted flux comparisons transfer the excluded-cylinder exponent to arch height and diameter tails.
- **claim-positive-pole-kernel-representation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-pv-pole-representation)): The weights $$v_A v_B Z_N(w),\qquad v_A=\Delta(A)^2\prod_{a\in A} a^{1-N},\qquad Z_N(w)=\frac1{N!}\int\Delta(s_1,\ldots,s_N)^2\prod_i dw(s_i)$$ with $\Delta$ Vandermonde have a finite, positive sum over $A,B$, and hence define a joint probability law after normalization. For this law,* <div class="samepage"> *$$\label{eq:pv:pole-representation} -R(-1)=\mathbb E_{A,B}\frac{K_N^w(0,-1)}{\pi_A(0)\pi_B(-1)}$$ and $\pi_A(0)=1$, $\sin\lambda/\lambda\le\pi_B(-1)\le1$. In particular all terms in the expectation are positive.* </div>
  - Explanation: Normalizes a positive summable law on pole subsets and represents the boundary evaluation as an expectation of positive polynomial kernels. The remaining denominator is uniformly bounded away from zero.
- **claim-low-pole-saturation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pv-pole-saturation)): Fix $0<\delta<1/2$ and put $J=N^{1-\delta}$. Under the law of Lemma <a href="#lem:pv:pole-representation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:pv:pole-representation">19</a>, let $\mathcal E_J$ be the event that $A$ and $B$ each contain every pole in their respective sets with index $l\le J$. Then $$\mathbb P(\mathcal E_J)=1-o(1).$$ For either subset, the probability of missing such a pole is $o(1)$ uniformly conditional on the other subset.
  - Explanation: A replacement comparison shows that all low poles are present with probability tending to certainty. The estimate is uniform when conditioning on the opposite subset, so independence is unnecessary.
- **claim-homogeneous-denominator-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-pv-denominator)): The homogeneous empty component satisfies ($D_0=2+\sqrt2,\ \delta=3/8$) $$S_{2n}(1,\ldots,1)\ge c\,D_0^{-n}(4D_0^2/9)^{n^2} n^{5/48}.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the homogeneous empty component below with its explicit exponential normalization and polynomial correction. Bounded logarithmic error is needed to retain the final observable exponent.
- **claim-planar-disk-localization** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-pv-planar-localization)): The disk partition function satisfies $$Z(R)=R^{1/12+o(1)}.$$
  - Explanation: Compares the balanced cylinder gas with planar disk gases after removing essential and oversized polygons at bounded multiplicative cost. The comparison gives the planar nesting exponent.
- **claim-weighted-incompatibility-degree** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pv-weighted-degree)): For every nonempty finite family $\mathcal H$ of available polygons, $$M_{\mathcal H}=\sum_{P\in\mathcal H}w(P),\qquad d_{\mathcal H} =\frac{\sum_{P,Q\in\mathcal H}w(P)I(P,Q)w(Q)}{M_{\mathcal H}} \le r_0^{-1}.$$ In particular, the bound does not depend on the circumference or the family.
  - Explanation: The common zero-free radius bounds the weighted average incompatibility degree of any finite available polygon family, independently of circumference. The proof uses connected logarithmic coefficients and even spectral moments.
- **claim-six-rotation-radius-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pv-six-rotations)): Let $V$ be the open Jordan interior of a honeycomb polygon $Q$ enclosing 0. Suppose that an open set $U$ contains $\omega^rV$ for $0\le r\le5$, and that $$U\cap(U+kp)=\varnothing\qquad(k\in\mathbb Z\setminus\{0\})$$ for $p\ge1$. If $D=\max_{z\in Q}\|z\|$, then $$D\le Cp\log(D+2)$$ with an absolute constant $C$.
  - Explanation: Bounds the radius of a polygon whose rotated interiors fit into an open set disjoint from its period translates. The conclusion includes a factor of the period and a logarithmic radius factor; it is not merely logarithmic growth in the period.
- **claim-oversized-period-free-polygon-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-pv-oversized-polygons)): Fix $\delta>0$. Suppose $\mathcal U$ is a family of planar honeycomb polygons enclosing 0 whose interiors are disjoint from their nontrivial translates by a common pure lattice period of length $p$. For all sufficiently large $p$, $$\sum_{\substack{P\in\mathcal U\\ \max_{z\in P}\|z\|>p^{1+\delta}}}x_*^{\|P\|}=O(1).$$ The constant is independent of the family and the period direction.
  - Explanation: Combines the geometric rotation obstruction with the weighted incompatibility bound to control the total critical weight of oversized period-free polygons. The bound is uniform over families and period directions.
- **claim-finite-positive-visit-recursion** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-pv-visits)): For every finite filled vertex set $D$ and $v\in D$, $$\begin{aligned} T_D(v)=3 &+\sum_{\substack{P\subset D\\v\in P}} 2x_*^{\|P\|-1}\big[-\cos\big(\sigma(2\pi-\tau_v(P))\big)\big] \label{eq:pv:visit-recursion}\\ &+\sum_{\substack{P\subset D\\v\in P^\circ}} 2x_*^{\|P\|}T_{P^\circ}(v).\notag \end{aligned}$$ Here $P^\circ$ is the strict interior vertex set and $\tau_v(P)\in\{\pi/3,-\pi/3\}$ is the counterclockwise turn at $v$. Every coefficient on the right is positive. Thus $T_D(v)\ge3$, it is monotone under inclusion of filled sets containing $v$, and there are absolute constants $c,C>0$ such that $$c\sum_{f\sim v}Z_D(f)\le T_D(v)\le C\sum_{f\sim v}Z_D(f).$$ If $D$ is cut out by admissible tiling lines in a convex polygon and $A_D(v)$ is the positive ordered excursion weight through $v$, then $$\cos(3\pi/8)A_D(v)\le T_D(v)\le A_D(v).$$
  - Explanation: An exact finite exploration identity recursively expands phased visit mass into positive polygon contributions. It implies filled-set monotonicity and comparison with incident-face polygon gases; comparison with absolute excursion mass additionally uses convex tiling-line boundaries.
- **claim-exponential-lateral-exit-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pv-lateral-tail)): Consider critical exits from a fixed bottom port in a strip of $h$ layers, or in a convex tiling-line truncation of that strip. Let $X(\gamma)$ be the largest horizontal distance of a visited vertex from the source. There are absolute constants $C,c>0$ such that $$\sum_{X(\gamma)\ge t}x_*^{\|\gamma\|} \le C\exp\{-ct/(h+1)\}\qquad(t\ge0).$$ The same bound, with a polynomial prefactor, holds with a first-length insertion. In particular the contribution with $X\ge h\log^2h$ to any first-length mass is smaller than every fixed inverse power of $h$ as $h\to\infty$.
  - Explanation: Separated flux rays give exponential suppression of lateral wandering uniformly in convex truncations. A geometric length bound extends the estimate to first-length mass and justifies logarithmic corridor confinement.
- **claim-strip-vertex-excursion-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pv-strip-visits)): Let $A_{S_h}(v)$ be the positive weight of ordered strip excursions through $v$, with both endpoints free on the two horizontal boundaries. Uniformly over strip vertices, $$A_{S_h}(v)\le h^{1/12+o(1)}.$$ For every fixed $\delta>0$, uniformly over vertices at least $\delta h$ layers from both horizontal boundaries, $$A_{S_h}(v)=h^{1/12+o(1)}.$$ The same upper bound holds for the ordered excursion weight $A_D(v)$ in every convex lateral truncation $D$, including excursions with lateral endpoints. Its lower bound holds uniformly when, in addition to the horizontal clearance above, $v$ is at distance at least $\delta h$ from every lateral side.
  - Explanation: Bounds ordered excursion weight through every strip vertex and gives a matching bulk exponent away from the walls. Convex truncations retain the upper bound, with lateral clearance required for their lower bound.
- **claim-deep-arch-translated-bridge-hits** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pv-translation-hits)): Let $\alpha$ be any same-side arch meeting the central third of the strip. Let $\mathcal H_h(\alpha)$ be the total weight of strip bridges, summed over all bottom sources, that intersect $\alpha$. For every fixed $\epsilon\in(0,1)$, $$\mathcal H_h(\alpha)\ge h^{3/4-\epsilon-o(1)},$$ uniformly over all such arches. In particular $\mathcal H_h(\alpha)\ge h^{3/4-o(1)}$ uniformly.
  - Explanation: Every arch reaching the central strip band is hit by sufficiently much translated bridge mass. Contact intervals, geometric alternatives and a second-moment estimate make the lower bound uniform over arches of unrestricted span and length.

```json
        "exact_quote": "The mass normalizing $\\mathbb P_R$ is $R^{3/4+o(1)}$. Uniformly at vertices in the concentric hexagon of side $R/3$, $$\\mathbb P_R(v\\in\\gamma)=R^{-2/3+o(1)}.$$ The corresponding upper bound holds at every vertex, and $\\mathbb E_R|\\gamma|=R^{4/3+o(1)}$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-pv-puncture",
        "exact_quote": "For every compact set $K\\subset\\{s\\in\\mathbb C:|\\Im s|<\\pi\\}$ there is $C_K<\\infty$ such that, for all $n\\ge1$ and $s\\in K$, $$|F_n(2\\cosh s)|\\le\n C_K n^{1/6+2\\Re(s^2)/(3\\pi^2)}.$$ There is a disk about $\\eta=0$, independent of $n$, in which $F_n$ has no zeros. For each fixed $k$, the order-$k$ Taylor coefficient of $\\log F_n$ at zero is $O_k(1+\\log n)$. At the positive fugacity two, $$F_n(2)=n^{1/6+o(1)}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-237.9"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-pv-flux",
        "exact_quote": "For an outer boundary port of a finite convex domain bounded by tiling lines, start a path inwards and let it exit anywhere. Then $$\\sum_\\gamma x_*^{|\\gamma|} e^{i\\sigma W(\\gamma)}=1,\\qquad \\sigma=3/8$$ where the sum is over the nontrivial walks to exit, $|\\gamma|$ counts vertices, and $W$ is total turning angle along honeycomb edges. Every such walk has $|W|\\le\\pi$, so its real phased weight is at least $\\cos(\\sigma\\pi)x_*^{|\\gamma|}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.9"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "source_id": "PM-237.9"
        }

        "exact_quote": "As rational identities, the maps from $V$ and $V^{\\otimes2}$, respectively, to $V^{\\otimes3}$ satisfy $$F_{12}(z)F_{23}(Tz)(C\\otimes1)=1\\otimes C,\\qquad\n F_{12}(z)F_{23}(t^2 z)(I\\otimes1)=(1\\otimes I)F(tz).$$ The checked matrices also satisfy the Yang–Baxter equation $$F_{12}(y)F_{23}(zy)F_{12}(z)\n =F_{23}(z)F_{12}(zy)F_{23}(y).$$",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-pv-row-relations",
        "exact_quote": "At fixed twist, the row transfers commute in their row arguments. Exchanging adjacent sites by $F$ intertwines the corresponding rows. At generic sites, and hence meromorphically, $$\\mathcal T_{\\kappa}(x_j)\\mathcal T_{\\kappa}(Tx_j)=1,\\qquad\n \\mathcal T_{\\kappa}(x_j)\\mathcal T_{\\kappa}(t^2x_j)=\\mathcal T_{\\kappa}(tx_j).$$ For adjacent sites $(x,Tx)$, insertion of $C$ intertwines the row with the row omitting those sites. For $(x,t^2x)$, insertion of $I$ intertwines it with the row having the single site $tx$. On charge zero, $\\mathcal T_\\kappa(0)=(1+\\kappa+\\kappa^{-1})1$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-pv-physical-eigenline",
        "exact_quote": "At $X=(1,\\ldots,1)$, $z=t$, and $\\kappa=\\pm i$, powers of the row transfer converge exponentially to a rank-one projection whose empty-to-empty entry is 1. In particular, eigenvalue 1 is simple and all other eigenvalues have modulus less than 1. The same conclusions hold at the reflected honeycomb point $z=t^2$.*\n\n*Near either set of physical sites and twist, the rows have a common analytic eigenline of charge zero. Its eigenvalue is rational in the row argument and analytic in the other data near regular arguments. At $\\kappa=\\pm i$ this row eigenvalue is identically 1.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-pv-vacuum",
        "exact_quote": "Use twist $\\zeta=t^2=i$. Let $\\rho X=(x_N,x_1,\\ldots,x_{N-1})$, and $\\Pi_\\kappa$ the corresponding rotation of spin slots with factor $\\kappa^{h_N}$ from the last (now first) spin. We construct a charge-zero vector polynomial $\\Psi_N(X)$ with $$F_{i,i+1}(x_i/x_{i+1})\\Psi(X)=\\Psi(s_i X),\\qquad\n \\Pi_\\zeta\\Psi(X)=\\Psi(\\rho X)$$ ($s_i$ adjacent transposition), homogeneous of degree $\\binom N2$, with degree at most $N-1$ in each variable. For a spin component $\\Psi_{N,\\boldsymbol h}$, the refined bounds are $$h_j=+1\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ \\deg_{x_j}\\Psi_{N,\\boldsymbol h}\\le N-2,\n \\qquad\n h_j=-1\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ x_j\\mid\\Psi_{N,\\boldsymbol h}.$$ It has the adjacent-pair reduction $$\\Psi_N(\\ldots,a,Ta,\\ldots)=s_C(a;Y)\\ \\iota_C \\Psi_{N-2}(Y),\n \\qquad\n s_C(a;Y)=(1+T)a\\prod_{x\\in Y}(x-t^5a)(x-t^6a),$$ where $Y$ is the remaining list and $\\iota_C$ inserts a $C$ tensor in the indicated positions; and at last parameter zero its projection onto last spin zero is $(\\prod_{j<N}x_j)\\Psi_{N-1}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.9"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-pv-vacuum",
          "exact_quote": "Use twist $\\zeta=t^2=i$. Let $\\rho X=(x_N,x_1,\\ldots,x_{N-1})$, and $\\Pi_\\kappa$ the corresponding rotation of spin slots with factor $\\kappa^{h_N}$ from the last (now first) spin. We construct a charge-zero vector polynomial $\\Psi_N(X)$ with $$F_{i,i+1}(x_i/x_{i+1})\\Psi(X)=\\Psi(s_i X),\\qquad\n \\Pi_\\zeta\\Psi(X)=\\Psi(\\rho X)$$ ($s_i$ adjacent transposition), homogeneous of degree $\\binom N2$, with degree at most $N-1$ in each variable. For a spin component $\\Psi_{N,\\boldsymbol h}$, the refined bounds are $$h_j=+1\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ \\deg_{x_j}\\Psi_{N,\\boldsymbol h}\\le N-2,\n \\qquad\n h_j=-1\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ x_j\\mid\\Psi_{N,\\boldsymbol h}.$$ It has the adjacent-pair reduction $$\\Psi_N(\\ldots,a,Ta,\\ldots)=s_C(a;Y)\\ \\iota_C \\Psi_{N-2}(Y),\n \\qquad\n s_C(a;Y)=(1+T)a\\prod_{x\\in Y}(x-t^5a)(x-t^6a),$$ where $Y$ is the remaining list and $\\iota_C$ inserts a $C$ tensor in the indicated positions; and at last parameter zero its projection onto last spin zero is $(\\prod_{j<N}x_j)\\Psi_{N-1}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.9"

      "exact_statement": "Use twist $\\zeta=t^2=i$. Let $\\rho X=(x_N,x_1,\\ldots,x_{N-1})$, and $\\Pi_\\kappa$ the corresponding rotation of spin slots with factor $\\kappa^{h_N}$ from the last (now first) spin. We construct a charge-zero vector polynomial $\\Psi_N(X)$ with $$F_{i,i+1}(x_i/x_{i+1})\\Psi(X)=\\Psi(s_i X),\\qquad\n \\Pi_\\zeta\\Psi(X)=\\Psi(\\rho X)$$ ($s_i$ adjacent transposition), homogeneous of degree $\\binom N2$, with degree at most $N-1$ in each variable. For a spin component $\\Psi_{N,\\boldsymbol h}$, the refined bounds are $$h_j=+1\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ \\deg_{x_j}\\Psi_{N,\\boldsymbol h}\\le N-2,\n \\qquad\n h_j=-1\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ x_j\\mid\\Psi_{N,\\boldsymbol h}.$$ It has the adjacent-pair reduction $$\\Psi_N(\\ldots,a,Ta,\\ldots)=s_C(a;Y)\\ \\iota_C \\Psi_{N-2}(Y),\n \\qquad\n s_C(a;Y)=(1+T)a\\prod_{x\\in Y}(x-t^5a)(x-t^6a),$$ where $Y$ is the remaining list and $\\iota_C$ inserts a $C$ tensor in the indicated positions; and at last parameter zero its projection onto last spin zero is $(\\prod_{j<N}x_j)\\Psi_{N-1}$.",
      "explanation": "Constructs the charge-zero polynomial vacuum by interpolation at cup and zero nodes. Refined spin-dependent degree bounds force the remaining exchange and cyclic discrepancies to vanish.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-pv-vacuum",
          "exact_quote": "Use twist $\\zeta=t^2=i$.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.9"
        }
      ],

      "kind": "construction",

          "source_id": "PM-237.9"
        }
      ],

        "exact_quote": "Put $D(a,b)=a^2+\\sqrt2 ab+b^2$ and $K(a,b)=(a^2-b^2)/D(a,b)$. It satisfies $$S_N(X^{-1})=\\frac{S_N(X)}{\\prod_i x_i^{N-1}}$$ and the second fusion identity $$S_N(a,t^2a,Y)=\\sqrt2\\,t a\\prod_{y\\in Y}(y-t^5 a)\\ S_{N-1}(ta,Y).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-237.9"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-pv-pairing-interpolation",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $\\kappa\\ne0$ and put $\\eta=\\kappa+\\kappa^{-1}$. The family $X_{n,m}$ is uniquely determined among rational functions by the following properties. It is symmetric separately in its two blocks, and $S_N^2X_{n,m}$ is polynomial of degree at most $2(N-1)$ in each site, where $N=n+m$. Within block A its reductions are $$\\begin{aligned}\n X_{n,m}(a,Ta,A';B)&=X_{n-2,m}(A';B),\\\\\n X_{n,m}(a,t^2a,A';B)&=X_{n-1,m}(ta,A';B),\\\\\n \\lim_{a\\to0\\text{ or }\\infty}X_{n,m}(a,A';B)\n &=X_{n-1,m}(A';B),\n\\end{aligned}$$ The pair reductions require $n\\ge2$, and the extreme reduction requires $n\\ge1$; the analogous rules hold in block B with $m$ in place of $n$. The initial value is $X_{0,0}=1$, and at every balanced size $n=m>0$, $$X_{n,n}(a_1,\\ldots,a_n;Ta_n,\\ldots,Ta_1)=(1+\\eta)^n.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-237.9"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-pv-pairing-interpolation",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $\\kappa\\ne0$ and put $\\eta=\\kappa+\\kappa^{-1}$. The family $X_{n,m}$ is uniquely determined among rational functions by the following properties. Within block A its reductions are $$\\begin{aligned}\n X_{n,m}(a,Ta,A';B)&=X_{n-2,m}(A';B),\\\\\n X_{n,m}(a,t^2a,A';B)&=X_{n-1,m}(ta,A';B),\\\\\n \\lim_{a\\to0\\text{ or }\\infty}X_{n,m}(a,A';B)\n &=X_{n-1,m}(A';B),\n\\end{aligned}$$ The pair reductions require $n\\ge2$, and the extreme reduction requires $n\\ge1$; the analogous rules hold in block B with $m$ in place of $n$. The initial value is $X_{0,0}=1$, and at every balanced size $n=m>0$, $$X_{n,n}(a_1,\\ldots,a_n;Ta_n,\\ldots,Ta_1)=(1+\\eta)^n.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-237.9"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Fix $\\kappa\\ne0$ and put $\\eta=\\kappa+\\kappa^{-1}$. The family $X_{n,m}$ is uniquely determined among rational functions by the following properties. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
```
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **boundary port** ([source](#sec:pv:introduction)): A *port* is the midpoint of an edge cut by a tiling line.
- **visited-vertex path length** ([source](#sec:pv:introduction)): A path between ports visits distinct honeycomb vertices, and its length $\|\gamma\|$ is their number.
- **critical bridge law** ([source](#sec:pv:introduction)): The bridge law assigns each such path probability $x_*^{\|\gamma\|}/b_h$.
- **subpower exponent notation** ([source](#sec:pv:introduction)): $$ All three sums are finite. These exponents are unchanged if the paths are also confined to horizontal distance $h\log^2h$ from their initial port.* </div> Throughout, $f(h)=h^{a+o(1)}$ means that $f(h)>0$ for all sufficiently large $h$ and $\log f(h)/\log h\to a$.
- **same-side arch** ([source](#sec:pv:introduction)): An *arch* returns to the wall where it started.
- **separator polygon polynomial** ([source](#pm-h-4)): Let $F_n(\eta)$ sum over systems of pairwise vertex-disjoint unoriented simple polygons, each separating the two marks.
- **tensor charge** ([source](#pm-h-9)): The charge of a tensor is the sum of its spins.
- **bilinear transpose convention** ([source](#pm-h-9)): Transpose throughout this construction involves no conjugation.
- **twisted row transfer** ([source](#pm-h-10)): For $X=(x_1,\ldots,x_N)$ and $\kappa\ne0$, bring an auxiliary slot of parameter $z$ past the sites in their listed order and trace its output against its input, weighting the identified spin $h$ by $\kappa^h$.
- **normalized polynomial vacuum** ([source](#pm-h-18)): Let $S_N$ be the empty component, which is not identically zero by cup reduction, and put $\Omega=\Psi/S_N$.
- **planar surrounding-polygon gas** ([source](#c01-c1-localization-of-surrounding-polygons)): Write $Z(R)$ for the planar gas at fugacity 2 of mutually disjoint honeycomb polygons enclosing the face center 0 and contained within distance $R$ of it.
- **filled vertex set** ([source](#c01-c1-the-two-leg-mass)): A finite vertex set $D$ is *filled* if every lattice vertex strictly inside a simple polygon in its induced honeycomb graph also belongs to $D$.
- **finite excursion** ([source](#c01-c1-the-two-leg-mass)): An excursion joins two distinct boundary ports and visits distinct vertices of $D$.
- **ordered excursion convention** ([source](#c01-c1-the-two-leg-mass)): We order its endpoints, so reversal gives a second excursion.
- **phased vertex-visit sum** ([source](#c01-c1-the-two-leg-mass)): For $v\in D$, define $$T_D(v)=\sum_{\gamma\ni v}x_*^{\|\gamma\|}e^{i\sigma W(\gamma)}, \qquad \sigma=3/8.$$
- **macroscopic hexagon arc** ([source](#sec:pv:hexagon-proof)): Say an arc is macroscopic when its diameter is at least $R/10$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-critical-vertex-activity | x_* | = | $(2\cos(\pi/8))^{-1}=(2+\sqrt2)^{-1/2}$ | Weight per visited vertex at criticality. |
| q-strip-bridge-mass | b_h | = | $h^{-1/4+o(1)}$ | Integer layer height tending to infinity. |
| q-strip-first-length-mass | \ell_h | = | $h^{13/12+o(1)}$ | Integer layer height tending to infinity. |
| q-strip-mean-length | \mathbb E_h\|\gamma\| | = | $h^{4/3+o(1)}$ | Free terminal port; optional horizontal confinement as stated in the theorem. |
| q-hexagon-normalizing-mass | Mass normalizing \mathbb P_R | = | $R^{3/4+o(1)}$ | Integer hexagon side tending to infinity. |
| q-hexagon-bulk-visit-probability | \mathbb P_R(v\in\gamma) | = | $R^{-2/3+o(1)}$ | Matching exponent in the concentric inner hexagon; upper bound throughout. |
| q-hexagon-mean-length | \mathbb E_R\|\gamma\| | = | $R^{4/3+o(1)}$ | Integer hexagon side tending to infinity. |
| q-separator-complex-growth | \|F_n(2\cosh s)\| | ≤ | $C_K n^{1/6+2\Re(s^2)/(3\pi^2)}$ | Compact twist sets strictly inside the stated complex strip. |
| q-positive-separator-growth | F_n(2) | = | $n^{1/6+o(1)}$ | Balanced cylinder size tending to infinity at positive fugacity two. |
| q-flux-spin | \sigma | = | $3/8$ | Turning phase of nontrivial critical exits. |
| q-strip-flux-normalization | b_h+\cos(\sigma\pi)A_h | = | $1$ | Arch and bridge masses use critical vertex weights. |
| q-cylinder-exponential-tail | b_h^{(N)}+A_\infty^{(N)}-A_h^{(N)} | ≤ | $C_N e^{-c_N h}$ | Height varies at fixed circumference; no uniformity in circumference is claimed. |
| q-rightward-half-plane-arch-mass | Half-plane arch mass to the right | = | $1/(2\cos(3\pi/8))$ | Positive endpoint displacement. |
| q-homogeneous-empty-component-lower-bound | S_{2n}(1,\ldots,1) | ≥ | $c\,D_0^{-n}(4D_0^2/9)^{n^2} n^{5/48}$ | Positive unspecified lower-bound constant. |
| q-planar-nesting-growth | Z(R) | = | $R^{1/12+o(1)}$ | Disk radius tending to infinity. |
| q-weighted-incompatibility-upper-bound | d_{\mathcal H} | ≤ | $r_0^{-1}$ | Uniform in family and circumference. |
| q-six-rotation-radius | D | ≤ | $Cp\log(D+2)$ | Radius and pure translation period as in the geometric lemma. |
| q-lateral-critical-exit-tail | \sum_{X(\gamma)\ge t}x_*^{\|\gamma\|} | ≤ | $C\exp\{-ct/(h+1)\}$ | Uniform lateral tail with height-dependent decay scale. |
| q-strip-bulk-excursion-growth | A_{S_h}(v) | = | $h^{1/12+o(1)}$ | Vertices at least the specified fixed fractional height from both walls. |
| q-translated-bridge-hitting-mass | \mathcal H_h(\alpha) | ≥ | $h^{3/4-\epsilon-o(1)}$ | The height limit is taken with the positive epsilon fixed. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-finite-flux** [claim](#stmt-lem-pv-flux) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pv-flux): Balance unused-vertex extensions, cancel repeated-vertex polygon tails by reversal, and control exit phases by convexity. -> Exact finite boundary flux and positive comparison.
- **step-strip-flux** [claim](#stmt-cor-pv-strip-flux) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-pv-strip-flux): Exhaust the strip by convex cuts, extend side exits to eliminate their mass, and split high arches into bridge pairs. -> Strip normalization, finiteness and crude bridge bounds.
- **step-cylinder-decay** [claim](#stmt-lem-pv-cylinder-decay) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pv-cylinder-decay): Compare indecomposable bridges with their plane lifts, then use finite connectivity states and rational generating functions to strengthen summability to exponential decay. -> Exponential convergence of cylindrical path sums.
- **step-local-relations** [claim](#stmt-lem-pv-local-relations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pv-local-relations): Project tensor contractions onto the local channels, verify scalar rational identities, and interpolate the cleared Yang–Baxter discrepancy at degeneration values. -> Local slides and checked Yang–Baxter identity.
- **step-row-relations** [claim](#stmt-lem-pv-row-relations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pv-row-relations): Move auxiliary crossings through the row and take the twisted trace; use rank reductions for auxiliary and site fusions. -> Commuting rows and row/site fusion relations.
- **step-physical-eigenline** [claim](#stmt-lem-pv-physical-eigenline) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pv-physical-eigenline): Cancel closed loops, bound crossing arcs by cylinder decay, factor the limiting boundary amplitudes, and continue the simple physical eigenline analytically. -> Normalized physical projection and common analytic eigenline.
- **step-multiaffine-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-auto-claim-1) / [proof](#proof-stmt-auto-claim-1): Eliminate the cup channel by denominator divisibility, transfer adjacent bit patterns with invertible channel maps, and rule out all sorted support patterns. -> Vanishing criterion for the cyclic discrepancy.
- **step-polynomial-vacuum** [claim](#stmt-prop-pv-vacuum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pv-vacuum): Interpolate cup values and a zero node, prove refined spectator bounds and compatible reductions, then eliminate the last exchange and cyclic discrepancies. -> Polynomial charge-zero vacuum with degree, exchange, cyclic and reduction properties.
- **step-empty-pfaffian** [claim](#stmt-prop-pv-empty-component) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pv-empty-component): Use geometric empty normalization for symmetry, cancel alternating denominators in the Pfaffian expression, and identify it by interpolation and reductions. -> Symmetric empty-component formula, reciprocity and second fusion.
- **step-normalized-reductions** [claim](#stmt-cor-pv-normalized-reductions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-pv-normalized-reductions): Cancel matching scalar factors for cup insertion, use the generic unique row eigenline for fusion, and use triangularity for extreme-site limits. -> Normalized cup, fusion and extreme reductions.
- **step-physical-pairing** [claim](#stmt-prop-pv-physical-pairing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pv-physical-pairing): Glue finite-height boundary states, track oriented seam intersections, cancel nonseparating polygons and pass coefficientwise to the infinite cylinder. -> Identification of the homogeneous pairing with the separator polynomial.
- **step-pairing-characterization** [claim](#stmt-prop-pv-pairing-interpolation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pv-pairing-interpolation): Establish reductions and balanced cup values, then divide a cleared difference by all forced factors and compare the remaining block degrees. -> Unique rational characterization of the pairing.
- **step-positive-pole-law** [claim](#stmt-lem-pv-pole-representation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pv-pole-representation): Expand test and trial determinants over their pole subsets, apply determinant integration, and identify the cofactor ratio with the polynomial evaluation kernel. -> Positive pole-subset law and kernel expectation.
- **step-low-pole-saturation** [claim](#stmt-lem-pv-pole-saturation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pv-pole-saturation): Replace a high occupied pole by a missing low pole, compare orthogonal-polynomial values and sum the resulting uniform replacement estimates. -> Uniform conditional saturation of low poles.
- **step-boundary-exponent** [claim](#stmt-prop-pv-boundary) / [proof](#c01-c1-the-boundary-exponent): Express excluded arch mass through a row derivative, bound its kernel above and below using saturated pole sets, and transfer the exponent to spatial and height tails by tilted flux. -> Bridge mass exponent and half-plane arch height and diameter exponents.
- **step-denominator-normalization** [claim](#stmt-prop-pv-denominator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pv-denominator): Take the homogeneous Pfaffian limit as a moment determinant, evaluate the reference norms, and bound the determinant ratio by a logarithmic trace estimate. -> Explicit lower normalization with bounded logarithmic error.
- **step-color-residue-representation** [claim](#stmt-thm-pv-puncture) / [proof](#c01-c1-color-formula-and-small-cycle-gas): Verify the neutral color product against the pairing characterization and convert selected label changes to residue objects with their exact interaction factors. -> Finite color formula and small-cycle integral representation.
- **step-radial-periodization** [claim](#stmt-thm-pv-puncture) / [proof](#c01-c1-radial-periodization-of-the-small-cycle-integral): Deform the kernels analytically in the radial nome, retain the zero-nome numerator, and reorganize contours with the prescribed residue cancellations. -> Analytic periodized numerator with controlled contour bookkeeping.
- **step-upper-circle-resummation** [claim](#stmt-thm-pv-puncture) / [proof](#c01-c1-upper-edge-resummation): Apply the winding circle determinant expansion and Gaussian linearization, keeping the count-dependent signs before replacing upper variables by holes. -> Residual defect expansion with explicit winding and count dependence.
- **step-defect-kernel-control** [claim](#stmt-thm-pv-puncture) / [proof](#c01-c1-a-kernel-positivity-decomposition): Separate a smoothed charge pole, repulsions and a positive regular matrix form; use coefficient moment tests to establish the needed frequency inequalities. -> Population suppression and charge-variance controls.
- **step-defect-assembly** [claim](#stmt-thm-pv-puncture) / [proof](#c01-c1-estimates-and-summation-of-the-defect-gas): Turn frequency bounds into spatial population estimates, deform contours jointly, sum the original count by Poisson summation and localize all remaining objects. -> Absolutely assembled defect sum bounded after explicit bulk and twist extraction.
- **step-complex-separator-bound** [claim](#stmt-thm-pv-puncture) / [proof](#c01-c1-bulk-calibration-and-puncture-exponent): Evaluate pressure and determinant growth, remove the analytic pressure correction before Cauchy extraction, divide by the squared empty normalization, and use analytic compactness and positivity. -> Complex growth estimate, uniform zero-free disk, logarithmic coefficient bounds and positive-fugacity exponent.
- **step-weighted-incompatibility** [claim](#stmt-lem-pv-weighted-degree) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pv-weighted-degree): Use same-sign connected logarithmic coefficients to bound weighted graph walks, then let even spectral moments grow at fixed family and cylinder. -> Circumference-independent weighted degree bound.
- **step-six-rotation-geometry** [claim](#stmt-lem-pv-six-rotations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pv-six-rotations): Exclude period-network contacts, classify a projected endpoint arc using an auxiliary torus, shorten its shear word by cone monodromy, and bound the additive radius cost of pure lifts. -> Radius bounded by the period times a logarithmic radius factor.
- **step-remove-oversized-polygons** [claim](#stmt-cor-pv-oversized-polygons) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-pv-oversized-polygons): Order polygon pairs by area, force an incompatible lattice rotation with the radius bound, and apply the weighted-degree estimate before finite-subset exhaustion. -> Bounded total mass of oversized period-free polygons.
- **step-planar-localization** [claim](#stmt-prop-pv-planar-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-pv-oversized-polygons-2): Bound essential polygon mass by incompatible families, remove essential and oversized polygons, and compare the cylinder gas with disjoint planar disk gases. -> Planar surrounding-polygon partition exponent.
- **step-positive-visit-recursion** [claim](#stmt-prop-pv-visits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pv-visits): Explore until exit or first repeat, cancel exterior-stem returns, and iterate the positive strict-interior recursion to compare incident-face gases. -> Exact visit recursion, monotonicity and convex-domain comparison.
- **step-lateral-tail** [claim](#stmt-lem-pv-lateral-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pv-lateral-tail): Subtract flux across staggered slanted cuts, exploit separation of the resulting rays and integrate the tail using the strip length bound. -> Exponential lateral tails with and without first-length insertion.
- **step-strip-visits** [claim](#stmt-lem-pv-strip-visits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pv-strip-visits): Use a transverse period to remove oversized enclosing polygons, compare retained gases with disks, apply finite visit comparison and remove distant lateral sources. -> Uniform strip visit upper bound and bulk matching exponent.
- **step-mixed-first-moment** [claim](#stmt-prop-pv-mixed) / [proof](#c01-c1-a-fixed-source-strip-version): Count visits in finite truncations, average over horizontal width, discard lateral sources and use a central band that forces the height condition. -> Conditioned mixed mass, first-length mass and normalized mean.
- **step-hexagon-chords** [claim](#stmt-thm-pv-hexagon) / [proof](#sec:pv:hexagon-proof): Normalize macroscopic chords using boundary mass and lateral tails, compare bulk visits with inscribed disk gases and sum vertex probabilities. -> Hexagon normalizer, central visit exponent and mean length.
- **step-tripod-pair-comparison** [claim](#stmt-thm-pv-bridge) / [proof](#pm-h-60): Represent first contacts by marked tripods, use deletion balances and positive nesting monotonicity, and compare phased expressions with positive intersecting pair sums. -> Bridge–arch pair sum controlled by bridge–bridge pairs and marked bridge mass.
- **step-translation-hits** [claim](#stmt-lem-pv-translation-hits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pv-translation-hits): Use equal-height contact intervals for spread or displaced arch prefixes; in the confined case combine tube suppression with a bridge-layer second-moment argument. -> Uniform translated bridge hitting lower bound.
- **step-bridge-first-moment** [claim](#stmt-thm-pv-bridge) / [proof](#pm-h-63): Combine the tripod comparison with the lateral bridge-pair upper bound and translated hitting lower bound, recover marked bridge visit mass and sum horizontal translation orbits. -> Bridge first-length exponent, normalized mean and corridor preservation.

```json
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-pv-pole-saturation",

Under the law of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:pv:pole-representation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:pv:pole-representation\">19</a>, let $\\mathcal E_J$ be the event that $A$ and $B$ each contain every pole in their respective sets with index $l\\le J$. Then $$\\mathbb P(\\mathcal E_J)=1-o(1).$$ For either subset, the probability of missing such a pole is $o(1)$ uniformly conditional on the other subset.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.9"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "The sharp power uses asymptotic pole saturation and successive limits in comparison parameters, rather than a finite-size error certificate.",
      "step_id": "step-boundary-exponent"
    },

          "source_id": "PM-237.9"

      ],
      "reason": "The matching positive-fugacity lower exponent uses locally uniform subsequential analytic limits and positivity; the common zero-free radius is asserted without an explicit numerical value.",

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-pv-weighted-degree",
          "exact_quote": "Now freeze both $\\mathcal H$ and its containing cylinder, and let odd $k$ tend to infinity.",

        }
      ],
      "reason": "The proof freezes a finite family and its cylinder before taking arbitrarily high spectral moments; fixed-order logarithmic coefficient bounds alone are not a finite certification procedure for the uniform degree bound.",

  "pm_id": "PM-237.9",
  "schema": "pm.roadmap.v2",
  "steps": [
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-pv-flux",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-pv-flux",
          "exact_quote": "For an outer boundary port of a finite convex domain bounded by tiling lines, start a path inwards and let it exit anywhere. Then $$\\sum_\\gamma x_*^{|\\gamma|} e^{i\\sigma W(\\gamma)}=1,\\qquad \\sigma=3/8$$ where the sum is over the nontrivial walks to exit, $|\\gamma|$ counts vertices, and $W$ is total turning angle along honeycomb edges. Every such walk has $|W|\\le\\pi$, so its real phased weight is at least $\\cos(\\sigma\\pi)x_*^{|\\gamma|}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.9"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-finite-flux",
      "inputs": [
        "Critical vertex activity",
        "Turning phase",
        "Finite convex tiling-line domain"
      ],
      "method": "Balance unused-vertex extensions, cancel repeated-vertex polygon tails by reversal, and control exit phases by convexity.",
      "output": "Exact finite boundary flux and positive comparison.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-pv-flux"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-cor-pv-strip-flux",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-finite-flux"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-pv-strip-flux",
          "exact_quote": "The strip masses $A_h,b_h$ are finite and satisfy $$\\label{eq:pv:strip-flux}\n b_h+\\cos(\\sigma\\pi)A_h=1.$$ Moreover, $$A_{h+1}-A_h\\le x_*^{-1} b_{h+1}^2,\n \\qquad \\frac{c}{h+1}\\le b_h\\le1.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-237.9"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-strip-flux",
      "inputs": [
        "Finite boundary flux",
        "Positive critical path weights"
      ],
      "method": "Exhaust the strip by convex cuts, extend side exits to eliminate their mass, and split high arches into bridge pairs.",
      "output": "Strip normalization, finiteness and crude bridge bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-cor-pv-strip-flux"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-pv-cylinder-decay",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-strip-flux"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-pv-cylinder-decay",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $N$. Let $b_h^{(N)}$ and $A_h^{(N)}$ denote the cylindrical versions of the strip masses from a fixed bottom port, with terminal port summed on the top and bottom cut respectively. Then $\\sum_h b_h^{(N)}<\\infty$, the limit $A_\\infty^{(N)}$ is finite, and there are $C_N,c_N>0$ such that $$b_h^{(N)}+A_\\infty^{(N)}-A_h^{(N)}\n \\le C_N e^{-c_N h}.$$ The same bounds apply when a terminal port is prescribed instead of summed.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.9"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-cylinder-decay",
      "inputs": [
        "Strip upper bound",
        "Fixed circumference",
        "Finite connectivity state space"
      ],
      "method": "Compare indecomposable bridges with their plane lifts, then use finite connectivity states and rational generating functions to strengthen summability to exponential decay.",
      "output": "Exponential convergence of cylindrical path sums.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-pv-cylinder-decay"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-pv-local-relations",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-pv-local-relations",
          "exact_quote": "As rational identities, the maps from $V$ and $V^{\\otimes2}$, respectively, to $V^{\\otimes3}$ satisfy $$F_{12}(z)F_{23}(Tz)(C\\otimes1)=1\\otimes C,\\qquad\n F_{12}(z)F_{23}(t^2 z)(I\\otimes1)=(1\\otimes I)F(tz).$$ The checked matrices also satisfy the Yang–Baxter equation $$F_{12}(y)F_{23}(zy)F_{12}(z)\n =F_{23}(z)F_{12}(zy)F_{23}(y).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-237.9"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-local-relations",
      "inputs": [
        "Checked matrix",
        "Cup tensor",
        "Fusion embedding",
        "Channel projectors"
      ],
      "method": "Project tensor contractions onto the local channels, verify scalar rational identities, and interpolate the cleared Yang–Baxter discrepancy at degeneration values.",
      "output": "Local slides and checked Yang–Baxter identity.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-pv-local-relations"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-pv-row-relations",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-local-relations"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-pv-row-relations",
          "exact_quote": "At fixed twist, the row transfers commute in their row arguments. Exchanging adjacent sites by $F$ intertwines the corresponding rows. At generic sites, and hence meromorphically, $$\\mathcal T_{\\kappa}(x_j)\\mathcal T_{\\kappa}(Tx_j)=1,\\qquad\n \\mathcal T_{\\kappa}(x_j)\\mathcal T_{\\kappa}(t^2x_j)=\\mathcal T_{\\kappa}(tx_j).$$ For adjacent sites $(x,Tx)$, insertion of $C$ intertwines the row with the row omitting those sites. For $(x,t^2x)$, insertion of $I$ intertwines it with the row having the single site $tx$. On charge zero, $\\mathcal T_\\kappa(0)=(1+\\kappa+\\kappa^{-1})1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.9"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-row-relations",
      "inputs": [
        "Local slides",
        "Yang–Baxter identity",
        "Charge conservation"
      ],
      "method": "Move auxiliary crossings through the row and take the twisted trace; use rank reductions for auxiliary and site fusions.",
      "output": "Commuting rows and row/site fusion relations.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-pv-row-relations"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-pv-physical-eigenline",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-cylinder-decay",
        "step-row-relations"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-pv-physical-eigenline",
          "exact_quote": "At $X=(1,\\ldots,1)$, $z=t$, and $\\kappa=\\pm i$, powers of the row transfer converge exponentially to a rank-one projection whose empty-to-empty entry is 1. In particular, eigenvalue 1 is simple and all other eigenvalues have modulus less than 1. The same conclusions hold at the reflected honeycomb point $z=t^2$.*\n\n*Near either set of physical sites and twist, the rows have a common analytic eigenline of charge zero. Its eigenvalue is rational in the row argument and analytic in the other data near regular arguments. At $\\kappa=\\pm i$ this row eigenvalue is identically 1.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.9"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-physical-eigenline",
      "inputs": [
        "Cylinder convergence",
        "Commuting rows",
        "Physical twist and honeycomb specialization"
      ],
      "method": "Cancel closed loops, bound crossing arcs by cylinder decay, factor the limiting boundary amplitudes, and continue the simple physical eigenline analytically.",
      "output": "Normalized physical projection and common analytic eigenline.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-pv-physical-eigenline"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-auto-claim-1",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-local-relations"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-auto-claim-1",
          "exact_quote": "A tensor polynomial multiaffine in at least three consecutive variables, with the bit-dependent spin support and exchange relation just stated, is zero.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.9"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-multiaffine-vanishing",
      "inputs": [
        "Spin-dependent support",
        "Multiaffine degree restriction",
        "Discrepancy exchange relation"
      ],
      "method": "Eliminate the cup channel by denominator divisibility, transfer adjacent bit patterns with invertible channel maps, and rule out all sorted support patterns.",
      "output": "Vanishing criterion for the cyclic discrepancy.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-auto-claim-1"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-pv-vacuum",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-local-relations",
        "step-multiaffine-vanishing"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-pv-vacuum",
          "exact_quote": "Use twist $\\zeta=t^2=i$. Let $\\rho X=(x_N,x_1,\\ldots,x_{N-1})$, and $\\Pi_\\kappa$ the corresponding rotation of spin slots with factor $\\kappa^{h_N}$ from the last (now first) spin. We construct a charge-zero vector polynomial $\\Psi_N(X)$ with $$F_{i,i+1}(x_i/x_{i+1})\\Psi(X)=\\Psi(s_i X),\\qquad\n \\Pi_\\zeta\\Psi(X)=\\Psi(\\rho X)$$ ($s_i$ adjacent transposition), homogeneous of degree $\\binom N2$, with degree at most $N-1$ in each variable. For a spin component $\\Psi_{N,\\boldsymbol h}$, the refined bounds are $$h_j=+1\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ \\deg_{x_j}\\Psi_{N,\\boldsymbol h}\\le N-2,\n \\qquad\n h_j=-1\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ x_j\\mid\\Psi_{N,\\boldsymbol h}.$$ It has the adjacent-pair reduction $$\\Psi_N(\\ldots,a,Ta,\\ldots)=s_C(a;Y)\\ \\iota_C \\Psi_{N-2}(Y),\n \\qquad\n s_C(a;Y)=(1+T)a\\prod_{x\\in Y}(x-t^5a)(x-t^6a),$$ where $Y$ is the remaining list and $\\iota_C$ inserts a $C$ tensor in the indicated positions; and at last parameter zero its projection onto last spin zero is $(\\prod_{j<N}x_j)\\Psi_{N-1}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.9"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-polynomial-vacuum",
      "inputs": [
        "Lower-size polynomial vacua",
        "Local slides",
        "Multiaffine vanishing criterion"
      ],
      "method": "Interpolate cup values and a zero node, prove refined spectator bounds and compatible reductions, then eliminate the last exchange and cyclic discrepancies.",
      "output": "Polynomial charge-zero vacuum with degree, exchange, cyclic and reduction properties.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-pv-vacuum"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-pv-empty-component",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-polynomial-vacuum",
        "step-physical-eigenline"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-pv-empty-component",
          "exact_quote": "Put $D(a,b)=a^2+\\sqrt2 ab+b^2$ and $K(a,b)=(a^2-b^2)/D(a,b)$. The symmetric empty component is $$S_N(X)=\\prod_{i<j}\\frac{D(x_i,x_j)}{x_i-x_j}\\ {\\rm Pf}[K(x_i,x_j)]$$ where for odd size an extra last Pfaffian index has $K_{i,N+1}=1$, $i\\le N$, corresponding to parameter zero. It satisfies $$S_N(X^{-1})=\\frac{S_N(X)}{\\prod_i x_i^{N-1}}$$ and the second fusion identity $$S_N(a,t^2a,Y)=\\sqrt2\\,t a\\prod_{y\\in Y}(y-t^5 a)\\ S_{N-1}(ta,Y).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-237.9"
        }

      "id": "step-empty-pfaffian",
      "inputs": [

        "Cup reduction and zero-node data"

      "method": "Use geometric empty normalization for symmetry, cancel alternating denominators in the Pfaffian expression, and identify it by interpolation and reductions.",
      "output": "Symmetric empty-component formula, reciprocity and second fusion.",

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-cor-pv-normalized-reductions",
          "exact_quote": "At generic parameters, the normalized states reduce by insertion of $C$ at adjacent sites $(a,Ta)$, and by insertion of $I$ at $(a,t^2a)$, replacing those sites by the single site $ta$. When one parameter tends to zero or infinity, the spin-zero component at that slot tends to the reduced $\\Omega$, with scalar factor one.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.9"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-normalized-reductions",
      "inputs": [
        "Empty-component normalization",
        "Row intertwining",
        "Generic eigenline uniqueness"

      "method": "Cancel matching scalar factors for cup insertion, use the generic unique row eigenline for fusion, and use triangularity for extreme-site limits.",
      "output": "Normalized cup, fusion and extreme reductions.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-cor-pv-normalized-reductions"
    },
    {

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-pv-physical-pairing",
          "exact_quote": "For two homogeneous blocks of equal size $n$, $$X_{n,n}(1,\\ldots,1;\\kappa)=F_n(\\kappa+\\kappa^{-1}).$$ Here the left side is the bilinear vacuum contraction just defined and the right side is the positive polygon polynomial from Section <a href=\"#sec:pv:introduction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:pv:introduction\">1</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.9"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-physical-pairing",
      "inputs": [
        "Normalized physical states",
        "Twisted block contraction",
        "Positive finite-height polygon sums"
      ],
      "method": "Glue finite-height boundary states, track oriented seam intersections, cancel nonseparating polygons and pass coefficientwise to the infinite cylinder.",
      "output": "Identification of the homogeneous pairing with the separator polynomial.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-pv-physical-pairing"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-pv-pairing-interpolation",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-normalized-reductions"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-pv-pairing-interpolation",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $\\kappa\\ne0$ and put $\\eta=\\kappa+\\kappa^{-1}$. The family $X_{n,m}$ is uniquely determined among rational functions by the following properties. It is symmetric separately in its two blocks, and $S_N^2X_{n,m}$ is polynomial of degree at most $2(N-1)$ in each site, where $N=n+m$. Within block A its reductions are $$\\begin{aligned}\n X_{n,m}(a,Ta,A';B)&=X_{n-2,m}(A';B),\\\\\n X_{n,m}(a,t^2a,A';B)&=X_{n-1,m}(ta,A';B),\\\\\n \\lim_{a\\to0\\text{ or }\\infty}X_{n,m}(a,A';B)\n &=X_{n-1,m}(A';B),\n\\end{aligned}$$ The pair reductions require $n\\ge2$, and the extreme reduction requires $n\\ge1$; the analogous rules hold in block B with $m$ in place of $n$. The initial value is $X_{0,0}=1$, and at every balanced size $n=m>0$, $$X_{n,n}(a_1,\\ldots,a_n;Ta_n,\\ldots,Ta_1)=(1+\\eta)^n.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-237.9"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-pairing-characterization",
      "inputs": [
        "Normalized reductions",
        "Empty-component reciprocity",
        "Block symmetry"
      ],
      "method": "Establish reductions and balanced cup values, then divide a cleared difference by all forced factors and compare the remaining block degrees.",
      "output": "Unique rational characterization of the pairing.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-pv-pairing-interpolation"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-pv-pole-representation",

        "step-row-relations",
        "step-physical-eigenline"

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-pv-pole-representation",
          "exact_quote": "The weights $$v_A v_B Z_N(w),\\qquad v_A=\\Delta(A)^2\\prod_{a\\in A} a^{1-N},\\qquad\n Z_N(w)=\\frac1{N!}\\int\\Delta(s_1,\\ldots,s_N)^2\\prod_i dw(s_i)$$ with $\\Delta$ Vandermonde have a finite, positive sum over $A,B$, and hence define a joint probability law after normalization. For this law,*\n\n<div class=\"samepage\">\n\n\n*$$\\label{eq:pv:pole-representation}\n -R(-1)=\\mathbb E_{A,B}\\frac{K_N^w(0,-1)}{\\pi_A(0)\\pi_B(-1)}$$ and $\\pi_A(0)=1$, $\\sin\\lambda/\\lambda\\le\\pi_B(-1)\\le1$. In particular all terms in the expectation are positive.*\n\n</div>",
          "source_id": "PM-237.9"
        }

    },

Under the law of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:pv:pole-representation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:pv:pole-representation\">19</a>, let $\\mathcal E_J$ be the event that $A$ and $B$ each contain every pole in their respective sets with index $l\\le J$. Then $$\\mathbb P(\\mathcal E_J)=1-o(1).$$ For either subset, the probability of missing such a pole is $o(1)$ uniformly conditional on the other subset.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.9"
        }

          "exact_quote": "For critical bridges across $h$ layers from a fixed bottom port to all top ports, $b_h=h^{-1/4+o(1)}$. The half-plane arch mass to the right is $1/(2\\cos(3\\pi/8))$; its height and diameter tails both have exponent $-1/4$.",

          "exact_quote": "#### Upper-edge resummation\n\nThe upper contours still contain an arbitrarily large number of points. The following circle determinant expansion sums them first and replaces them by holes on two separated contours.",
```

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-empty-component-pfaffian**: The symmetric empty component is represented by the displayed Pfaffian product, with an extra index at odd size. Polynomial cancellation permits evaluation at coincident sites.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-checks-prove-asymptotics**: Treat agreement on small empty components as certification of the bridge mean or separator growth exponent. -> Finite algebraic agreement does not establish the infinite-volume limits or the asymptotic estimates.
- **anti-divide-at-singular-sites**: Evaluate the normalized vacuum by dividing at arbitrary complex sites. -> The empty component may vanish, making the normalized rational expression undefined.
- **anti-drop-normalization**: Use growth of the unnormalized Pfaffian component as the growth of the separator observable. -> The numerator and denominator carry exponential factors that must cancel in the common normalization.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The empty-component proposition gives a finite Pfaffian construction, and the vacuum proof supplies independent initial polynomials. This executable checks that construction at small sizes, including removable singularities. It does not certify the asymptotic bridge, chord, or separator theorems.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with sites, a list of rational strings, of length at most 3.
# PRECONDITIONS: Each site is an exact rational; the empty list is allowed.
# OUTPUTS: JSON rational strings [u,v] representing u + v*sqrt(2).
# TIME: Finite symbolic expansion; no asymptotic runtime bound claimed.
# SPACE: Finite symbolic expressions; no asymptotic storage bound claimed.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic extended by sqrt(2).
# FAILURE: Invalid input or incomplete polynomial cancellation raises an error.
import json
import sys
import sympy as sp

root = sp.sqrt(2)

def pfaffian(matrix):
    n = len(matrix)
    if n == 0:
        return sp.Integer(1)
    result = sp.Integer(0)
    for j in range(1, n):
        keep = [k for k in range(n) if k not in (0, j)]
        minor = [[matrix[a][b] for b in keep] for a in keep]
        result += (-1)**(j + 1) * matrix[0][j] * pfaffian(minor)
    return result

def empty_component(n):
    xs = sp.symbols('x:' + str(n))
    size = n + n % 2
    matrix = [[sp.Integer(0) for _ in range(size)] for _ in range(size)]
    prefactor = sp.Integer(1)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            a, b = xs[i], xs[j]
            denominator = a*a + root*a*b + b*b
            matrix[i][j] = (a*a - b*b) / denominator
            matrix[j][i] = -matrix[i][j]
            prefactor *= denominator / (a - b)
    if n % 2:
        for i in range(n):
            matrix[i][n] = sp.Integer(1)
            matrix[n][i] = -matrix[i][n]
    polynomial = sp.cancel(prefactor * pfaffian(matrix), extension=root)
    polynomial = sp.expand(polynomial)
    if sp.denom(polynomial) != 1:
        raise ValueError('uncancelled polynomial denominator')
    return xs, polynomial

payload = json.load(sys.stdin)
assert set(payload) == {'sites'}
raw = payload['sites']
assert isinstance(raw, list) and len(raw) <= 3
assert all(isinstance(x, str) for x in raw)
sites = [sp.Rational(x) for x in raw]
xs, polynomial = empty_component(len(sites))
value = sp.expand(polynomial.subs(dict(zip(xs, sites)), simultaneous=True))
v = sp.simplify(value.coeff(root))
u = sp.simplify(value - v*root)
assert u.is_Rational and v.is_Rational
print(json.dumps([str(u), str(v)]))
```

```json
    "reason": "The empty-component proposition gives a finite Pfaffian construction, and the vacuum proof supplies independent initial polynomials. This executable checks that construction at small sizes, including removable singularities. It does not certify the asymptotic bridge, chord, or separator theorems.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON object with sites, a list of rational strings, of length at most 3.\n# PRECONDITIONS: Each site is an exact rational; the empty list is allowed.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON rational strings [u,v] representing u + v*sqrt(2).\n# TIME: Finite symbolic expansion; no asymptotic runtime bound claimed.\n# SPACE: Finite symbolic expressions; no asymptotic storage bound claimed.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic extended by sqrt(2).\n# FAILURE: Invalid input or incomplete polynomial cancellation raises an error.\nimport json\nimport sys\nimport sympy as sp\n\nroot = sp.sqrt(2)\n\ndef pfaffian(matrix):\n    n = len(matrix)\n    if n == 0:\n        return sp.Integer(1)\n    result = sp.Integer(0)\n    for j in range(1, n):\n        keep = [k for k in range(n) if k not in (0, j)]\n        minor = [[matrix[a][b] for b in keep] for a in keep]\n        result += (-1)**(j + 1) * matrix[0][j] * pfaffian(minor)\n    return result\n\ndef empty_component(n):\n    xs = sp.symbols('x:' + str(n))\n    size = n + n % 2\n    matrix = [[sp.Integer(0) for _ in range(size)] for _ in range(size)]\n    prefactor = sp.Integer(1)\n    for i in range(n):\n        for j in range(i + 1, n):\n            a, b = xs[i], xs[j]\n            denominator = a*a + root*a*b + b*b\n            matrix[i][j] = (a*a - b*b) / denominator\n            matrix[j][i] = -matrix[i][j]\n            prefactor *= denominator / (a - b)\n    if n % 2:\n        for i in range(n):\n            matrix[i][n] = sp.Integer(1)\n            matrix[n][i] = -matrix[i][n]\n    polynomial = sp.cancel(prefactor * pfaffian(matrix), extension=root)\n    polynomial = sp.expand(polynomial)\n    if sp.denom(polynomial) != 1:\n        raise ValueError('uncancelled polynomial denominator')\n    return xs, polynomial\n\npayload = json.load(sys.stdin)\nassert set(payload) == {'sites'}\nraw = payload['sites']\nassert isinstance(raw, list) and len(raw) <= 3\nassert all(isinstance(x, str) for x in raw)\nsites = [sp.Rational(x) for x in raw]\nxs, polynomial = empty_component(len(sites))\nvalue = sp.expand(polynomial.subs(dict(zip(xs, sites)), simultaneous=True))\nv = sp.simplify(value.coeff(root))\nu = sp.simplify(value - v*root)\nassert u.is_Rational and v.is_Rational\nprint(json.dumps([str(u), str(v)]))\n",
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The bridge scaling laws specify asymptotic mathematical behavior without a sampling implementation, finite-size acceptance threshold, or software correctness contract. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The critical path measure could motivate lattice simulations, but the paper supplies neither a sampler nor a dynamical update rule for generating trajectories. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Pfaffian expression offers symbolic evaluation data, but its displayed rational factors require cancellation or limiting evaluation at coincident sites; a robust general numerical evaluator is not specified. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite visit recursion constructs a terminating exact evaluation of phased visit mass by enumerating simple polygons and recursively evaluating their strict interiors. This realizes a finite identity without certifying the asymptotic bridge exponent. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The cylinder proof retains occupied ports and connectivity in finitely many states, but it does not specify a reusable state encoding or establish storage and update costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The transfer rows act on tensor spaces, but the construction gives no hardware mapping, precision allocation, or accelerator resource contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The zero-free disk controls a polygon generating polynomial and its incompatibilities; it supplies no cryptographic primitive or adversarial security property. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The physical eigenline is a transfer-matrix state near specified lattice parameters, with no learning objective, training procedure, or generalization guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The weighted incompatibility estimate bounds a polygon-family average, rather than constructing an optimal compatible family or solving a routing objective. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Commuting transfer rows and their algebraic intertwiners concern lattice partition states; no distributed execution, communication, or fault model is constructed. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The principal additional relevance is foundational statistical mechanics of critical honeycomb polymers. Its asymptotic laws do not alone construct an engineering model of material response or polymer processing. |
